# Agent harness-fit verification — axis-4 of convergence-gate

# TIER-3 evidence-trail provenance only: `.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota/memory/feedback_sota_researcher_harness_fit_verification_2026_05_02.md` carries the 5-instance evidence ladder + per-class analysis; this rule is the operational mechanism that file's evidence promotes.

## The rule

Sota-researcher / architect / general agent dispatches that surface SOTA candidates MUST include a **harness-fit verification step** asking "is this applicable to OUR specific architecture?" — beyond the convergence-gate Axis 1+2+3 (T1 sources / T2 practitioners / Axis 3 stability) that scores SOTA-status. Without harness-fit, candidates pass convergence-gate AND still fail at ship-target verification because of architectural mismatch invisible to upstream-source-only audit.

This is **convergence-gate Axis 4** (harness-fit) — separate from Axis 1+2+3 (which classify SOTA-status of the candidate ITSELF) and applied AFTER convergence-gate PASS, BEFORE ship-target verification.

## When this rule applies

Whenever an agent (sota-researcher / architect / general-purpose / Explore / code-reviewer / debugger) returns a ship-candidate ranking from a Wave-N dispatch:

1. The agent's convergence-gate verdict is necessary BUT NOT SUFFICIENT for ship-readiness
2. The candidate may be valid SOTA AND inapplicable to claude-sota
3. Apply the 4-axis harness-fit probe BEFORE writing T1 consult prompt OR queuing the ship

## How to apply (4-axis probe)

Before declaring an agent's ship-candidate "ready":

### Probe 1 — count-OVER

If the candidate claims a count delta (commit count, file count, line count, star count): verify against authoritative local source (`git log`, `wc -l`, `Glob | wc -l`, `mcp__github__search_repositories` star refresh) BEFORE accepting. Agent counts via remote APIs can drift, paginate-truncate, or include stale cache.

### Probe 2 — SDK-vs-CLI surface

If the candidate proposes adopting an SDK feature / config flag / env var: verify the **invocation surface** exists in claude-sota:
- Does claude-sota use the SDK or CLI? Check `.claude/hooks/scripts/*.py` + `.claude/settings.json` env block
- Does the candidate's mechanism map to claude-sota's actual mode (interactive vs print-mode vs SDK-managed-agent)?
- If the candidate requires a mode claude-sota doesn't run in, REJECT-for-fit even if SOTA-valid upstream

### Probe 3 — architectural-API

If the candidate proposes a primitive (cache_control, prompt-cache, structured-outputs, batch-API): verify the **API ecosystem match**:
- Anthropic-API primitive vs OpenAI-API primitive vs vendor-neutral OTel
- claude-sota's gpt5.5/codex hooks talk to OpenAI-API endpoints; Anthropic-only primitives don't apply there
- Cross-check before assuming "this Anthropic pattern works on codex"

### Probe 5 — mode-harness-shape

If the candidate proposes adopting a skill/workflow/pattern with operational mode dependencies (HARD-GATE on user interaction, interactive-vs-autonomous mismatch, meta-skill assumptions about sister-harness presence, file-count or size-sprawl orthogonal to existing layout): verify the **operational mode + harness shape match**:
- Does the candidate skill HARD-GATE on interactive user approval? Verify against claude-sota's autonomous /loop mode (incompatible)
- Does the candidate require a sister-skill or sister-harness present in upstream's framework but absent locally? (e.g., Superpowers TDD-for-skills test framework)
- Does verbatim port introduce file count or size disproportionate to existing layer? (e.g., a single skill bringing 7+ files when sibling skills are 1-3 files)
- If ANY mode/harness assumption is unmet, REJECT-FOR-FIT even if SOTA-valid upstream + Axis 1+2+3 PASS

### Probe 4 — plugin-namespace

If the candidate proposes vendoring an artifact from upstream (ECC, codex-plugin-cc, anthropics-cookbook): check **plugin-loaded skill namespace** FIRST:
- `everything-claude-code:<artifact>` exposed via plugin? → DUPLICATE if vendored, REJECT per kiss-dry-yagni Must-Never #4
- `codex:<artifact>` exposed via codex plugin? → DUPLICATE
- `<plugin>:<artifact>` only available as legacy-shim? → still plugin-exposed
- Only proceed with vendoring if the artifact is NOT in any loaded plugin namespace

**Where to check**: the available-skills list injected into agent context via system-reminder (lists every `<plugin>:<skill>` entry). Agents can also `mcp__github__get_file_contents` on the plugin's `marketplace.json` to enumerate exposed commands.

### Probe 6 — supplementary direct-file/registry blockers (FM-09 promotion iter-67)

If the candidate proposes adopting an upstream artifact (skill, MCP server, plugin, library, vendored pattern) whose viability depends on:
- License compatibility (claude-sota is permissive-license-only — MIT / Apache-2.0 / BSD acceptable; AGPLv3 / GPLv3 / SSPL / proprietary REJECT)
- README badge / status banner (archive-status, deprecation notice, "moved to maintenance mode" warning)
- Published-package existence (npm / PyPI / cargo / go-modules registry)
- Build-deps availability (Cargo / GCC / Clang / Python venv requirement)

VERIFY via direct-file/registry probes:
- **LICENSE file read** at upstream root: `cat Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/LICENSE | head -5` — match against permissive-license whitelist
- **README badge inspection**: `rg -n "img.shields.io/badge/license|maintenance|archived|deprecated" Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/README.md`
- **Registry direct-existence probe**: `curl -sS https://registry.npmjs.org/<pkg-encoded>` (`{"error":"Not found"}` = phantom) OR `curl https://pypi.org/pypi/<pkg>/json` (404 = phantom)
- **Build-deps probe**: `which cargo gcc clang` OR check `pyproject.toml` / `package.json` for unsupported dep stack

If ANY direct-file/registry probe surfaces a blocker, REJECT-FOR-FIT even if SOTA-valid upstream + Axis 1+2+3 PASS + Probe 1-5 PASS. The blocker is STRUCTURAL — no operational mitigation exists.

**Empirical evidence n=2 (FM-09 ladder)**: Memory/RAG audit (openviking AGPLv3 LICENSE blocker — `reference_memory_rag_audit_HNF_agplv3_blocker_2026_05_02.md:52-64`); Ship D (ast-grep MCP phantom @anthropic/mcp-ast-grep npm package — `reference_ship_d_ast_grep_phantom_npm_HNF_2026_05_02.md`).

### Probe 7 — demand-gate split (NEW 2026-05-03; .a/.b sub-class added 2026-05-05 Wave 27 per Agent E §Issue 1 catch-22)

If the candidate proposes adopting a primitive (MCP / skill / library / tool) that is structurally sound (Probes 1-6 PASS): verify the **harness-side operational driver** BEFORE adoption. Probe 7 splits into two outcomes — `.a` REJECT (demand-absence) and `.b` STUDY-PILOT eligible (demand-creates-new-workflow). The split closes the Wave 27 catch-22 surfaced at `tmp/wave27-agentE-saturation-adversarial-2026-05-05.md:46-56,89-106` (Probe 7.a as currently worded auto-REJECTs any primitive that creates a NEW workflow because no sss invocation surface yet exists — circular per Jeffries YAGNI canonical statement at `kiss-dry-yagni.md:31-33`).

**Hard precondition (before applying .a or .b)**: earlier Axis-4 blockers WIN. If the candidate duplicates an existing shipped primitive (Probe 4 plugin-namespace), collides with plugin namespace, requires an unmet harness/mode assumption (Probe 5 mode-harness-shape), fails Probe 6 (license/registry/build-deps blocker), is SUPERSEDED-BY-X (existing primitive owns the workflow), or is a META-HARNESS competing-framework shape per `docs/verified-avoid.md` Cohort 1, classify under that blocker instead. Probe 7.b is ONLY available for structurally sound primitives that create a genuinely new, non-duplicative workflow.

#### Probe 7.a — DEMAND-ABSENCE (REJECT-FOR-FIT)

What sss workflow / invocation surface / consumer would route through this primitive TODAY? Verify the 3-clause check:
- Cite a specific path (existing or queued)
- Is the candidate's value proposition met by an EXISTING shipped primitive? (e.g., "graphiti MCP already provides FalkorDB temporal KG access" → dbhub adds no marginal value AS A REPLACEMENT)
- Is the candidate displacing or extending? Displacement requires migration plan; extension requires marginal-value evidence

If NO current/queued workflow AND .b 5-clause check (below) does not survive, REJECT-FOR-FIT.a (demand-absence) — REJECT-decisive.

#### Probe 7.b — DEMAND-CREATES-NEW-WORKFLOW (STUDY-PILOT eligible)

A structurally sound primitive may create a genuinely new workflow that no existing sss primitive serves. STUDY-PILOT eligibility requires ALL 5 clauses to hold (codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.84 Q2 prescription at `.claude/state/codex_consult_ship27_3_probe7_demand_creates_OUT.txt`):

1. **Named operational use case**: cite the query/workflow and the consumer that would exercise it
2. **Cited local input/source path**: identify the concrete data, repo path, tool surface, or state file (e.g., `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/state/mcp_overhead_audit.jsonl`)
3. **Wiring path**: name the adapter/ETL/config needed to make the primitive consume the source; if wiring is non-trivial, it is part of the pilot cost
4. **Incumbent comparison**: explain why existing shipped primitives or simpler local commands (`rg`, `jq`, GitNexus, Langfuse MCP, graphiti, mcp-memory, one-off Python scripts) do not already satisfy the use case OR are materially worse
5. **Reversible time-box**: define owner, max pilot cost, 30-day success criterion, and retirement path

**Pilot failure**: retire to `disabledMcpjsonServers` if applicable and document in `docs/verified-avoid.md` under the matching failure cohort; create a dedicated retired-pilot / demand-not-realized cohort only after n>=2 same-shape recurrences. NOTE: `verified-avoid.md` Cohort 2 is specifically Probe 6 direct-file/registry blockers per `docs/verified-avoid.md:47-49`; failed demand-pilots do NOT belong there by default. Use the matching failure cohort for retirement (e.g., a pilot that failed because it duplicated an existing primitive retires to Cohort 1 META-HARNESS competing-frameworks).

**Example (dbhub Wave 27 caveat — ETL-explicit)**: bytebase/dbhub could enable SQL analytics over a generated SQLite audit warehouse derived from `.claude/state/*.jsonl`; the pilot must include the JSONL → SQLite ingestion path plus at least one committed query/report consumer. Without that ETL path, dbhub is NOT sufficient evidence for Probe 7.b — DBHub's official surface (`https://dbhub.ai/` + `bytebase/dbhub@72adfdcf` README + `server.json`) supports configured Postgres/MySQL/SQL Server/MariaDB/SQLite databases via SQL tools, NOT direct JSONL querying. Wave 27 codex T1 verified-via codex secondary probe of DBHub introduction page + TOML config docs + GitHub MCP-fetched README/server.json [VERIFIED 2026-05-05 via `.claude/state/codex_consult_ship27_3_probe7_demand_creates_OUT.txt`].

**Harness-fit discriminator for REJECT vs STUDY-PILOT outcomes** (renamed from "4-class harness-fit-FAIL discriminator" per Wave 27 codex T1 Q1 prescription — `.b` is non-fail; original Q3 prescription at `.claude/state/codex_consult_axis4_subclass_taxonomy_probe7_demand_absence_OUT.txt:3825-3829` 2026-05-03; .a/.b split per Wave 27 codex T1 verdict at `.claude/state/codex_consult_ship27_3_probe7_demand_creates_OUT.txt` 2026-05-05): use BEFORE classifying a verdict to ensure the right Probe is invoked.

- `DEMAND-ABSENCE.a` (Probe 7.a): no current, queued, or counterfactual workflow survives the named-use-case check — REJECT-FOR-FIT
- `DEMAND-CREATES-NEW-WORKFLOW.b` (Probe 7.b, NON-FAIL CLASS): structurally sound primitive creates a named, non-duplicative workflow; STUDY-PILOT eligible only after the 5-clause check passes
- `SUPERSEDED-BY-X` (related to Probes 4/5): a workflow exists, but an incumbent primitive already owns it or was selected by prior verdict (e.g., cognee → Graphiti L3 verdict)
- `PLUGIN-NAMESPACE` (Probe 4): the artifact already exists in a loaded plugin namespace
- `MODE-HARNESS-SHAPE` (Probe 5): candidate requires an operating mode or harness assumption sss does not satisfy

**Empirical evidence**: n=1 fresh self-observed (bytebase/dbhub REJECT-FOR-FIT 0.85, 2026-05-03 — Agent task output records "no application-DB query workflow"; existing DB-adjacent MCPs cover all live use cases) + n=1 retro corroboration (apollo-graphql per parent `Z:/claude/.claude/projects/Z--claude/memory/reference_mcp_disabled_rationale.md:20`). 4 historical disabled MCPs (claude-context/grafana/opik/local-judge) NOT counted under this Probe — they are CREDENTIAL-GATED-PENDING / A/B-PENDING / SUPERSEDED-BY-X / sss-config-drift distinct classes per the discriminator above.

**Wave 27 caveat (2026-05-05)**: dbhub may be DEMAND-CREATES-NEW-WORKFLOW.b for audit-log SQL analytics, but ONLY with an explicit JSONL → SQLite/Postgres ETL path. DBHub does not currently provide direct JSONL querying. The Wave 27 .b codification specifically opens a pilot pathway IF the operator commits to building the ETL surface — without it, dbhub still falls under Probe 7.a REJECT-FOR-FIT.

Anti-pattern: adopting a primitive because it's SOTA-valid + plugin-namespace-clean + license-compatible WITHOUT identifying a single sss workflow that consumes it. Result is MCP slot occupation without operational driver — historically the disabled-MCP cohort pattern (n=6 in `manifests/services.yaml:80-98` plus `.claude/settings.json:217` `disabledMcpjsonServers`).

Anti-pattern (Probe 7.b bypass — Wave 27 codification): claiming `.b` STUDY-PILOT eligibility for a candidate that fails any earlier Axis-4 blocker (PLUGIN-NAMESPACE / MODE-HARNESS-SHAPE / SUPERSEDED-BY-X / Probe 6 / META-HARNESS competing-framework shape per `docs/verified-avoid.md` Cohort 1). The `.b` escape hatch is admissible ONLY for structurally sound primitives — see Hard precondition above. Without the precondition gate, `.b` becomes a bypass for any attractive new primitive that duplicates `team-lead` / 12-agent fleet / T1-T7 lifecycle / etc.

### Codex-rescue verdict provenance: wrapper-only vs BRIDGE-MODE (Wave 17 fire-36 codification per real GPT-5.5 codex T1 fire-33 prescribed_edit #3)

Codex-rescue dispatches have TWO distinct execution modes that need explicit disclosure per `cross-model-consensus.md §Env-funneled subagent stand-in disclosure mandate`:

1. **codex-rescue Sonnet-wrapper-only**: agent runs entirely under `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=claude-sonnet-4-6` per `CLAUDE.local.md` ENV (f); does NOT bridge to codex CLI. Verdict origin = Sonnet stand-in. **Cross-model consensus NOT satisfied.** Requires 2nd-stage validation per FM-09 specialization above.

2. **codex-rescue BRIDGE-MODE**: agent's Sonnet wrapper invokes real GPT-5.5 via `codex exec --ephemeral -p deep-review-exec` subprocess (background or foreground+tee). Verdict origin = real GPT-5.5 codex CLI. **Cross-model consensus IS satisfied** for the codex-CLI verdict portion.

#### Disclosure shape (mandatory per fire-24 mandate)

- **Wrapper-only**: `STAND-IN per CLAUDE.local.md ENV (f); codex CLI not invoked; verdict is Sonnet stand-in only — orchestrator must file 2nd-stage validation if recommending ADOPT/STUDY`
- **BRIDGE-MODE**: `BRIDGE-MODE: codex-rescue Sonnet wrapper invoking real GPT-5.5 via codex CLI subprocess at <task-id>; verdict origin = codex CLI; cross-model gate satisfied`

#### IMPORTANT: BRIDGE-MODE does NOT waive Probe 4-7 harness-fit checks

Even in BRIDGE-MODE, codex-rescue STILL has the FM-09 abstract-pattern-reasoning blind-spot for plugin-namespace + mode-harness-shape + direct-file-blockers + demand-absence probes. The model-asymmetry is satisfied; the harness-fit-domain-knowledge gap is NOT. For ADOPT-NOW / STUDY-PILOT recommendations, ALWAYS run the full 6-probe DAG (Probes 1-7) regardless of dispatch mode.

#### Empirical evidence

- BRIDGE-MODE precedent: Wave 17 fire-25C codex-rescue dispatch successfully bridged to bg job `bc0yt5r9i` per `tmp/wave17-fire25-token-eff-synthesis-2026-05-04.md:88` (fire-25C verdict origin = real GPT-5.5)
- Wrapper-only precedent: pre-fire-25C codex-rescue dispatches in earlier sessions (FM-09 n=5 ladder) — all required 2nd-stage validation per the original FM-09 specialization above

### Codex-rescue blind-spot specialization (FM-09 promotion iter-67)

n=5 same-arc 2026-05-02 evidence ladder establishes that **codex-rescue agent (GPT-5.5 deep-dive variant) systematically returns ADOPT-NOW / STUDY-PILOT verdicts WITHOUT running Probe 4 / Probe 5 / Probe 6**. Pattern is agent-architecture-specific, not user-error: codex-rescue specializes in abstract-pattern reasoning (Axis 1+2+3 SOTA classification — T1 sources / T2 practitioners / Axis 3 stability) NOT repo-local-inventory reasoning (Axis 4 harness-fit). Different reasoning surfaces; codex-rescue's strength is the source of its blind-spot.

**The 2-stage validation contract** (n=5 base rate validated 100% on observable cases):

1. **Receive codex-rescue ADOPT-NOW / STUDY-PILOT verdict** on awesome-agentic-patterns / SOTA-catalog abstract-pattern candidates
2. **Spawn 2nd-stage harness-fit-aware agent** (`sota-researcher` / `architect` / `Explore` — **NOT codex-rescue intermediary** which has the same blind-spot)
3. **2nd-stage brief MUST INCLUDE explicit Probe 4 + Probe 5 + Probe 6 + Probe 7 mandate** (Probe 7 added 2026-05-03; historical FM-09 ladder at L103-127 was based on Probe 4-6 only — NOT retroactively reclassified per `port-note-discipline.md §6` anti-pattern "Do not rewrite historical commit bodies/snapshots") with file:line cite scaffold per `feedback_agent_team_brief_must_cite_sota_repos_at_file_line_2026_05_02.md`
4. **2nd-stage verdict OVERRIDES first-stage** if any probe fails — no consensus arbitration; harness-fit is veto authority over SOTA-classification
5. **HONEST-NON-FINDING is high-value output** per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` — REJECT-FOR-FIT verdicts save downstream T1+T2+ship+revert cycles
6. **2nd-stage FM-17 BLOCK protection** (per Wave 48 Agent A finding F-4 conf=0.85): if 2nd-stage harness-fit agent fails to return a verdict (FM-17 class — 429 / timeout / wedge / stream-watchdog stall): treat as BLOCK — first-stage codex-rescue ADOPT-NOW verdict CANNOT ship without completed 2nd-stage validation. Document under `.claude/rules/fm17-subagent-fleet-depletion.md` recovery in close-synthesis. Re-dispatch 2nd-stage when pool available OR perform orchestrator-direct Probe DAG 1-7 manual run. NEVER allow first-stage ADOPT to propagate as ship-decision under 2nd-stage FM-17 loss.

**Anti-pattern**: re-fire codex-rescue with "also run Probe 4+5+6" — codex-rescue's strength is abstract-pattern reasoning depth; doubling its scope dilutes that depth without compensating for the architectural blind-spot. **Spawn 2nd-stage agent in parallel; do NOT serialize codex-rescue with itself.**

**FM-09 evidence ladder (n=5 same-arc 2026-05-02)**:

| # | Ship | codex-rescue verdict | 2nd-stage agent | 2nd-stage verdict | Probe miss |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | F (Stop Hook Auto-Continue) | ADOPT-NOW conf=0.94 | sota-researcher | REJECT-FOR-FIT | Probe 4 plugin-namespace (3 Stop hooks + 2 ECC skills already cover) |
| 2 | G (Cross-Cycle Consensus Relay) | ADOPT-NOW conf=0.92 | architect | REJECT-FOR-FIT | Probe 4 + Probe 5 (5+ existing surfaces decompose CCCR; convergence-gate ALL 4 axes fail) |
| 3 | H (Memory Synthesis from Logs) | ADOPT-NOW conf=0.89 | (3-layer wrapper-chain opacity) | Pattern B HNF | wrapper-chain observability gap → recovery: skip codex-rescue intermediary |
| 4 | Memory/RAG audit (openviking) | sota-researcher STUDY-PILOT 0.78 | architect direct dispatch | REJECT-FOR-FIT 0.92 | Probe 6 LICENSE/badge (AGPLv3 license blocker) |
| 5 | D (ast-grep MCP) | sota-researcher STUDY-PILOT 0.85 | orchestrator pre-flight Probe 5b | REJECT-FOR-FIT (phantom package) | Probe 6 npm-registry direct-existence (phantom @anthropic/mcp-ast-grep) |

Base rate: 5/5 observable cases overridden by 2nd-stage harness-fit-aware audit (Ship H opaque variant counts as recovery-required, not observable disagreement). FM-09 base rate = 100% — codex-rescue ADOPT-NOW / STUDY-PILOT on abstract patterns SHOULD always be re-validated by 2-stage dispatch.

**Mechanics source memory**: `.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota/memory/feedback_fm09_codex_rescue_skipped_probe4_n2_2026_05_02.md` (full mechanics + iter-66 audit-trail correction).

## Anti-patterns

- **Trusting agent ranking verbatim without 4-axis probe** — refuted by n=5 same-arc REJECTs. The agent's PASS verdict is necessary but NOT sufficient.
- **Skipping probe 4 because "the upstream repo is local"** — local clone existence ≠ ship-readiness. ECC plugin EXPOSES many ECC artifacts as plugin-loaded skills; vendoring them again duplicates.
- **Promoting at n=1 without classifying the failure into one of the 7 sub-classes** — refuted by `codification-threshold.md` cycle-322 jurisdiction; class identification is what makes promotion durable.
- **Applying Axis 4 BEFORE Axis 1+2+3** — the order matters: SOTA classification is necessary upstream context; harness-fit is the downstream filter. A non-SOTA candidate doesn't need Axis 4 (already REJECTED).

Successor trigger: **An 8th distinct sub-class surfaces** (currently 7 classes; promote to skill-layer at sub-class n=5+ instances per class — Probe 5 currently at n=2 / Probe 6 at n=2 / Probe 7 at n=2, monitor for additional same-class instances)

- Anthropic CC ships first-party plugin-namespace-aware vendor primitive (would obviate this rule's manual probe)

Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\src\core-skills\bmad-party-mode\SKILL.md:2:name: bmad-party-mode
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\src\core-skills\bmad-party-mode\SKILL.md:3:description: 'Orchestrates group discussions between installed BMAD agents, enabling natural multi-agent conversations where each agent is a real subagent with independent thinking. Use when user requests party mode, wants multiple agent perspectives, group discussion, roundtable, or multi-agent conversation about their project.'
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\src\core-skills\bmad-party-mode\SKILL.md:6:# Party Mode
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\src\core-skills\bmad-party-mode\SKILL.md:8:Facilitate roundtable discussions where BMAD agents participate as **real subagents** — each spawned independently via the Agent tool so they think for themselves. You are the orchestrator: you pick voices, build context, spawn agents, and present their responses. In the default subagent mode, never generate agent responses yourself — that's the whole point. In `--solo` mode, you roleplay all agents directly.
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\src\core-skills\bmad-party-mode\SKILL.md:12:The whole point of party mode is that each agent produces a genuinely independent perspective. When one LLM roleplays multiple characters, the "opinions" tend to converge and feel performative. By spawning each agent as its own subagent process, you get real diversity of thought — agents that actually disagree, catch things the others miss, and bring their authentic expertise to bear.
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\src\core-skills\bmad-party-mode\SKILL.md:19:- `--solo` — Run without subagents. Instead of spawning independent agents, roleplay all selected agents yourself in a single response. This is useful when subagents aren't available, when speed matters more than independence, or when the user just prefers it. Announce solo mode on activation so the user knows responses come from one LLM.
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\src\core-skills\bmad-party-mode\SKILL.md:23:1. **Parse arguments** — check for `--model` and `--solo` flags from the user's invocation.
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\src\core-skills\bmad-party-mode\SKILL.md:35:    The resolver merges four layers in order: `_bmad/config.toml` (installer base, team-scoped), `_bmad/config.user.toml` (installer base, user-scoped), `_bmad/custom/config.toml` (team overrides), and `_bmad/custom/config.user.toml` (personal overrides). Each entry under `agents` is keyed by the agent's `code` and carries `name`, `title`, `icon`, `description`, `module`, and `team`. Build an internal roster of available agents from those fields.

Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\how-to\install-bmad.md:179:npx bmad-method install --yes --action update \
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\how-to\install-bmad.md:186:`--set <module>.<key>=<value>` lets you set any module config option non-interactively. It's repeatable and scales to every module — present and future. The flag is applied as a post-install patch: the installer runs its normal flow first, then `--set` upserts each value into `_bmad/config.toml` (team scope) or `_bmad/config.user.toml` (user scope), and into `_bmad/<module>/config.yaml` so declared values carry forward to the next install.
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\how-to\install-bmad.md:188:**Example — install bmm with explicit project knowledge and skill level:**
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\how-to\install-bmad.md:191:npx bmad-method install --yes \
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\how-to\install-bmad.md:201:npx bmad-method install --list-options bmm
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\how-to\install-bmad.md:204:`--list-options` (no argument) lists every key the installer can find locally — built-in modules (`core`, `bmm`) plus any currently cached official modules. The cache is per-machine and can be cleared, so previously installed officials won't appear on a fresh checkout or an ephemeral CI worker until they're installed again. Community and custom modules aren't enumerated here; read the module's `module.yaml` directly to see what keys it declares.
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\how-to\install-bmad.md:208:- **Routing.** The patch step looks for `[modules.<module>] <key>` (or `[core] <key>`) in `config.user.toml` first; if found there, it updates that file. Otherwise it writes to the team-scope `config.toml`. So user-scope keys (e.g. `core.user_name`, `bmm.user_skill_level`) end up in `config.user.toml` and team-scope keys end up in `config.toml`, matching the partition the installer uses.
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\how-to\install-bmad.md:210:- **Carry-forward, declared keys.** Values for keys declared in `module.yaml` survive subsequent installs because they're also written to `_bmad/<module>/config.yaml`, which the installer reads as the prompt default on the next run.
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\how-to\install-bmad.md:211:- **Carry-forward, undeclared keys.** A value for a key the module's schema doesn't declare lands in `config.toml` for the current install but won't be re-emitted on the next install (the manifest writer's schema-strict partition drops unknown keys). Re-pass `--set` if you need it sticky, or edit `_bmad/config.toml` directly.
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\how-to\install-bmad.md:213:- **Modules not in `--modules`.** Setting a value for a module you didn't include prints a warning and the value is dropped (no file gets created for an uninstalled module).
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\how-to\install-bmad.md:218:`--set` is a post-install patch, so it applies the same way regardless of action type. Under `bmad install --action quick-update` (or `--yes` against an existing install, where quick-update is the default), `--set` patches the central config files at the end just like a regular install.
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\how-to\install-bmad.md:222:Anonymous GitHub API calls are capped at 60/hour per IP. A single install hits the API once per external module to resolve the stable tag. Offices behind NAT, CI runner pools, and VPNs can collectively exhaust this.

Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\how-to\install-bmad.md:260:### A pinned install keeps upgrading
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\how-to\install-bmad.md:262:Pinned installs don't upgrade. Quick-update applies patches and minors on stable channel only; it won't touch `pinned` or `next`. Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\explanation\named-agents.md:80:Concrete example: a team commits a single file telling Amelia to always use the Context7 MCP tool for library docs and to fall back to Linear when a story isn't in the local epics list. Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\explanation\named-agents.md:82:There's also a second customization surface for *cross-cutting* concerns: the central `_bmad/config.toml` and `_bmad/config.user.toml` (both installer-owned, rebuilt from each module's `module.yaml`) plus `_bmad/custom/config.toml` (team, committed) and `_bmad/custom/config.user.toml` (personal, gitignored) for overrides. This is where the **agent roster** lives — the lightweight descriptors that roster consumers like `bmad-party-mode`, `bmad-retrospective`, and `bmad-advanced-elicitation` read to know who's available and how to embody them. Rebrand an agent org-wide with a team override; add fictional voices (Kirk, Spock, a domain expert persona) as personal experiments via the `.user.toml` override — without touching any skill folder. The per-skill file shapes how Mary *behaves* when she activates; the central config shapes how other skills *see* her when they look at the field.
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\explanation\named-agents.md:92:Most AI assistants today are either menus or prompts, and both shift cognitive load onto the user. Named agents plus customizable skills let you talk to a teammate who already knows the work, and let your organization shape that teammate without forking.

Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\vi-vn\how-to\customize-bmad.md:212:**Yêu cầu**: Python 3.11+ vì các phiên bản cũ hơn không có `tomllib`. Không cần `pip install`, không cần `uv`, không cần virtualenv. Bạn có thể kiểm tra bằng `python3 --version`. Trên một số nền tảng, `python3` mặc định vẫn là 3.10 hoặc thấp hơn, nên có thể bạn sẽ phải cài 3.11+ riêng.
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\vi-vn\how-to\customize-bmad.md:288:`customize.toml` theo từng skill bao phủ **hành vi sâu** như hook, menu, `persistent_facts`, override persona cho một agent hay workflow đơn lẻ. Một bề mặt khác sẽ bao phủ **trạng thái cắt ngang** như các câu trả lời lúc cài đặt và roster agent mà những skill bên ngoài như `bmad-party-mode`, `bmad-retrospective` và `bmad-advanced-elicitation` sử dụng. Bề mặt đó nằm trong bốn file TOML ở root dự án:
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\vi-vn\how-to\customize-bmad.md:291:_bmad/config.toml               (do installer quản lý) team scope:   câu trả lời lúc cài đặt + agent roster
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\vi-vn\how-to\customize-bmad.md:292:_bmad/config.user.toml          (do installer quản lý) user scope:   user_name, language, skill level
Z:\repos\deps\BMAD-METHOD\docs\vi-vn\how-to\customize-bmad.md:317:- `_bmad/config.toml` và `_bmad/config.user.toml` sẽ **được tạo lại sau mỗi lần cài đặt** từ những câu trả lời mà installer thu thập. Hãy coi chúng là output chỉ đọc; mọi chỉnh sửa trực tiếp sẽ bị ghi đè ở lần cài tiếp theo. ---
name: bmad-party-mode
description: 'Orchestrates group discussions between installed BMAD agents, enabling natural multi-agent conversations where each agent is a real subagent with independent thinking. Use when user requests party mode, wants multiple agent perspectives, group discussion, roundtable, or multi-agent conversation about their project.'
---

# Party Mode

Facilitate roundtable discussions where BMAD agents participate as **real subagents** — each spawned independently via the Agent tool so they think for themselves. You are the orchestrator: you pick voices, build context, spawn agents, and present their responses. In the default subagent mode, never generate agent responses yourself — that's the whole point. In `--solo` mode, you roleplay all agents directly.

## Why This Matters

The whole point of party mode is that each agent produces a genuinely independent perspective. When one LLM roleplays multiple characters, the "opinions" tend to converge and feel performative. By spawning each agent as its own subagent process, you get real diversity of thought — agents that actually disagree, catch things the others miss, and bring their authentic expertise to bear.

## Arguments

Party mode accepts optional arguments when invoked:

- `--model <model>` — Force all subagents to use a specific model (e.g. `--model haiku`, `--model opus`). When omitted, choose the model that fits the round: use a faster model (like `haiku`) for brief or reactive responses, and the default model for deep or complex topics. Match model weight to the depth of thinking the round requires.
- `--solo` — Run without subagents. Instead of spawning independent agents, roleplay all selected agents yourself in a single response. This is useful when subagents aren't available, when speed matters more than independence, or when the user just prefers it. Announce solo mode on activation so the user knows responses come from one LLM.

## On Activation

1. **Parse arguments** — check for `--model` and `--solo` flags from the user's invocation.

2. Load config from `{project-root}/_bmad/core/config.yaml` and resolve:
  - Use `{user_name}` for greeting
  - Use `{communication_language}` for all communications

3. **Resolve the agent roster** by running:

    ```bash
    python3 {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/resolve_config.py --project-root {project-root} --key agents
    ```

    The resolver merges four layers in order: `_bmad/config.toml` (installer base, team-scoped), `_bmad/config.user.toml` (installer base, user-scoped), `_bmad/custom/config.toml` (team overrides), and `_bmad/custom/config.user.toml` (personal overrides). Each entry under `agents` is keyed by the agent's `code` and carries `name`, `title`, `icon`, `description`, `module`, and `team`. Build an internal roster of available agents from those fields.

4. **Load project context** — search for `**/project-context.md`. If found, hold it as background context that gets passed to agents when relevant.

5. **Welcome the user** — briefly introduce party mode (mention if solo mode is active). Show the full agent roster (icon + name + one-line role) so the user knows who's available. Ask what they'd like to discuss.

## The Core Loop

For each user message:

### 1. Pick the Right Voices

Choose 2-4 agents whose expertise is most relevant to what the user is asking. Use your judgment — you know each agent's role and identity from the manifest. Some guidelines:

- **Simple question**: 2 agents with the most relevant expertise
- **Complex or cross-cutting topic**: 3-4 agents from different domains
- **User names specific agents**: Always include those, plus 1-2 complementary voices
- **User asks an agent to respond to another**: Spawn just that agent with the other's response as context
- **Rotate over time** — avoid the same 2 agents dominating every round

### 2. Build Context and Spawn

For each selected agent, spawn a subagent using the Agent tool. Each subagent gets:

**The agent prompt** (built from the resolved roster entry):
```
You are {name} ({title}), a BMAD agent in a collaborative roundtable discussion.

## Your Persona
{icon} {name} — {description}

## Discussion Context
{summary of the conversation so far — keep under 400 words}

{project context if relevant}

## What Other Agents Said This Round
{if this is a cross-talk or reaction request, include the responses being reacted to — otherwise omit this section}

## The User's Message
{the user's actual message}

## Guidelines
- Respond authentically as {name}. Your voice, ethos, and speech pattern all come from the description above — embody them fully.
- Start your response with: {icon} **{name}:**
- Speak in {communication_language}.
- Scale your response to the substance — don't pad. If you have a brief point, make it briefly.
- Disagree with other agents when your perspective tells you to. Don't hedge or be polite about it.
- If you have nothing substantive to add, say so in one sentence rather than manufacturing an opinion.
- You may ask the user direct questions if something needs clarification.
- Do NOT use tools. Just respond with your perspective.
```

**Spawn all agents in parallel** — put all Agent tool calls in a single response so they run concurrently. If `--model` was specified, use that model for all subagents. Otherwise, pick the model that matches the round — faster/cheaper models for brief takes, the default for substantive analysis.

**Solo mode** — if `--solo` is active, skip spawning. Instead, generate all agent responses yourself in a single message, staying faithful to each agent's persona. Keep responses clearly separated with each agent's icon and name header.

### 3. Present Responses

Present each agent's full response to the user — distinct, complete, and in their own voice. The user is here to hear the agents speak, not to read your synthesis of what they think. Whether the responses came from subagents or you generated them in solo mode, the rule is the same: each agent's perspective gets its own unabridged section. Never blend, paraphrase, or condense agent responses into a summary.

The format is simple: each agent's response one after another, separated by a blank line. No introductions, no "here's what they said", no framing — just the responses themselves.

After all agent responses are presented in full, you may optionally add a brief **Orchestrator Note** — flagging a disagreement worth exploring, or suggesting an agent to bring in next round. Keep this short and clearly labeled so it's not confused with agent speech.

### 4. Handle Follow-ups

The user drives what happens next. Common patterns:

| User says... | You do... |
|---|---|
| Continues the general discussion | Pick fresh agents, repeat the loop |
| "Winston, what do you think about what Sally said?" | Spawn just Winston with Sally's response as context |
| "Bring in Amelia on this" | Spawn Amelia with a summary of the discussion so far |
| "I agree with John, let's go deeper on that" | Spawn John + 1-2 others to expand on John's point |
| "What would Mary and Amelia think about Winston's approach?" | Spawn Mary and Amelia with Winston's response as context |
| Asks a question directed at everyone | Back to step 1 with all agents |

The key insight: you can spawn any combination at any time. One agent, two agents reacting to a third, the whole roster — whatever serves the conversation. Each spawn is cheap and independent.

## Keeping Context Manageable

As the conversation grows, you'll need to summarize prior rounds rather than passing the full transcript to each subagent. Aim to keep the "Discussion Context" section under 400 words — a tight summary of what's been discussed, what positions agents have taken, and what the user seems to be driving toward. Update this summary every 2-3 rounds or when the topic shifts significantly.

## When Things Go Sideways

- **Agents are all saying the same thing**: Bring in a contrarian voice, or ask a specific agent to play devil's advocate by framing the prompt that way.
- **Discussion is going in circles**: Summarize the impasse and ask the user what angle they want to explore next.
- **User seems disengaged**: Ask directly — continue, change topic, or wrap up?
- **Agent gives a weak response**: Don't retry. Present it and let the user decide if they want more from that agent.

## Exit

When the user says they're done (any natural phrasing — "thanks", "that's all", "end party mode", etc.), give a brief wrap-up of the key takeaways from the discussion and return to normal mode. Don't force exit triggers — just read the room.

# Retrospective Workflow

**Goal:** Post-epic review to extract lessons and assess success.

**Your Role:** Developer facilitating retrospective.
- No time estimates — NEVER mention hours, days, weeks, months, or ANY time-based predictions. AI has fundamentally changed development speed.
- Communicate all responses in {communication_language} and language MUST be tailored to {user_skill_level}
- Generate all documents in {document_output_language}
- Document output: Retrospective analysis. Concise insights, lessons learned, action items. User skill level ({user_skill_level}) affects conversation style ONLY, not retrospective content.
- Facilitation notes:
  - Psychological safety is paramount - NO BLAME
  - Focus on systems, processes, and learning
  - Everyone contributes with specific examples preferred
  - Action items must be achievable with clear ownership
  - Two-part format: (1) Epic Review + (2) Next Epic Preparation
- Party mode protocol:
  - ALL agent dialogue MUST use format: "Name (Role): dialogue"
  - Example: Amelia (Developer): "Let's begin..."
  - Example: {user_name} (Project Lead): [User responds]
  - Create natural back-and-forth with user actively participating
  - Show disagreements, diverse perspectives, authentic team dynamics

## Conventions

- Bare paths resolve from the skill root.
- `{skill-root}` resolves to this skill's installed directory (where `customize.toml` lives).
- `{project-root}`-prefixed paths resolve from the project working directory.
- `{skill-name}` resolves to the skill directory's basename.

## On Activation

### Step 1: Resolve the Workflow Block

Run: `python3 {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/resolve_customization.py --skill {skill-root} --key workflow`

**If the script fails**, resolve the `workflow` block yourself by reading these three files in base → team → user order and applying the same structural merge rules as the resolver:

1. `{skill-root}/customize.toml` — defaults
2. `{project-root}/_bmad/custom/{skill-name}.toml` — team overrides
3. `{project-root}/_bmad/custom/{skill-name}.user.toml` — personal overrides

Any missing file is skipped. Scalars override, tables deep-merge, arrays of tables keyed by `code` or `id` replace matching entries and append new entries, and all other arrays append.

### Step 2: Execute Prepend Steps

Execute each entry in `{workflow.activation_steps_prepend}` in order before proceeding.

### Step 3: Load Persistent Facts

Treat every entry in `{workflow.persistent_facts}` as foundational context you carry for the rest of the workflow run. Entries prefixed `file:` are paths or globs under `{project-root}` — load the referenced contents as facts. All other entries are facts verbatim.

### Step 4: Load Config

Load config from `{project-root}/_bmad/bmm/config.yaml` and resolve:

- `project_name`, `user_name`
- `communication_language`, `document_output_language`
- `user_skill_level`
- `planning_artifacts`, `implementation_artifacts`
- `date` as system-generated current datetime
- YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`

### Step 5: Greet the User

Greet `{user_name}`, speaking in `{communication_language}`.

### Step 6: Execute Append Steps

Execute each entry in `{workflow.activation_steps_append}` in order.

Activation is complete. Begin the workflow below.

**Considering installation options**

I need to think about how to run the external installer for project configuration and skills, specifically for `_bmad`. It seems like there’s a marketplace plugin option too. The user provides the install path using “npx bmad-method install,” emphasizing it's a custom installer instead of the marketplace add. I’m guessing there’s a concern about security and supply chain risks versus using the pure marketplace add. It likely poses a P2 or P6 risk, but it shouldn't block the installation.
**Considering prescribed edits**

I need to decide on the "prescribed_edits." Since the verdict is not marked as NEEDS-REVISION, it should probably be an empty array, right? The schema mentions this field is only required for those marked as NEEDS-REVISION. But if it’s required, I could still include the empty array just to stay compliant. It’s a bit confusing, but I think that’s the best approach!
